# Decisions log

Decisions taken on the founder's behalf by the product-owner agent during the autonomous
build (`docs/notes/autonomous-build.md`). Newest first. Each entry: date · module ·
decision · reason · spec ID.

## Where the build stopped

Phase 1 ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES at 31c1923; Phase 2 at e060f98 (phase2-acceptance.md); Phase 3 (6.15a) at 397c1e8 (phase3-acceptance.md); 6.12 ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES at 639d06b; 6.14 increment 1 ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES at 7b92b98. Now: 6.13 Part A (step 0: 64-C4-b, 612-C1/C2 homes, 614-C1/C2/C4/C5), then 6.14 inc 2, 6.13 Part B, then 6.16, 6.17 (docs/notes/plans/phase3-6-rulings.md). Founder's open questions held for the end; full review cycle (option 1); typecheck before every commit.

**Environment caveat for the founder.** This session ran on PostgreSQL 16 (no Docker) and
could not download Prisma's native schema engine (binaries.prisma.sh is blocked by the
session's network policy). Migrations were generated and applied with Prisma's npm-hosted
WASM schema engine (same engine version, e922089) through `@prisma/adapter-pg`, and every
migration folder was proved by rebuilding a throwaway database from it and diffing
against `schema.prisma` (empty diff). Before relying on them, run `npx prisma migrate
dev` on a normal machine against Postgres 17 and confirm it reports no drift.

**Resolved 2026-10-01 (local machine).** With the native Prisma 7.9.1 engine on PostgreSQL
17.10, `prisma migrate deploy` applied all 23 migration folders (through
`20261001011312_phase1_closeout`) to a fresh throwaway database with none failed or rolled
back, and `prisma migrate diff --from-config-datasource --to-schema prisma/schema.prisma`
reported an empty migration (exit 0). The migrations are good on the native toolchain.

## Decisions

- 2026-10-03 · 6.13 · Part A run 1 residuals after the re-review of the fix round (80a4138), 72a832c..6d83abe. D1 (72a832c, flagged 6.1/6.9): the boundary switch's help now says what the resolver does: an amount edge is flipped only exactly on the tender floor (policy.boundaryValue, which the save requires to equal the tender floor), for the regime bands and a rule with followsBoundarySwitch true; every other amount edge is (min, max]; every percentage edge follows the switch (EN only: the matrix editor has no Malay hints). boundary-words.test.ts:17 now pins the true sentence and refuses "every amount and percentage band edge" (as-ruled change to the review-3 pin), plus new resolver tests that the words match inBand, regimeFor and pctBandMatch. D2 (51e411e): the Site tab's possession preview passes soFrom from the S.O. assignments (soFromOf), so the drawer is never offered where the act refuses NO_SO; soFrom is required in possessionFaults (the demo loader's call passes it too). D3 (5c36c10): /projects reads In Delay in O(contracts) rows (delay-batch.ts): per possessed contract, groupBy the highest approved revision decided on or before today and the latest progress day on or before today (one record a day per contract), then only those rows, grouped into Maps; identical readings tested against the earlier loader's rule over 60 generated sets reaching every reading state. Consider items, all done: the S.O. moved with a corrected Contract Date is resolved again on the new date on the matrix in force that day, at the value it was resolved at (c2ef354, flagged 6.12): same office moves and records the rule it now rests on, audited with before/after and resolvedAgainOn; no matrix, no single S.O. or another office refuses the correction before anything is written, naming why (SO_MOVE_NO_MATRIX / SO_MOVE_UNRESOLVED / SO_MOVE_OTHER_OFFICE, worded once in supervision). ASSUMED only on a reading that rests on the straight line, behind or on track (f3dc6d4, flagged against 6.12's Projects list): not possessed, not set, not judged and a lapsed date for completion carry no tag; the Projects list uses the shared ProvenanceBadge through a DataList chip's new optional provenance (the shared component extended, its word in search and CSV), and the header counts those on the straight-line reading. A cleared award sum insured clears its stored reason (6545a55, flagged 6.11). An issued award with an unexplained difference reads "issued before a reason was required" (d204924, flagged 6.11; noReasonText, both places). One derivation of the works figure (6d83abe, flagged 6.11): appendixMinimum reads baselineOf's worksMinimum through appendixInsured; worksMinimum is required in Baseline; appendixMinimum, governingSum and governingSums drop the awarded value. Existing tests changed: boundary-words.test.ts (as ruled, D1); review-screens.test.ts (the "(assumed reading)" pin replaced by the ProvenanceBadge pin, as ruled); possession.test.ts (the shared fixture gains an S.O. in force before, fixture-only, as soFrom is now required); insurance-terms.test.ts (15 calls drop the awarded-value argument, signature only, every assertion unchanged). Gates: npm test 448 files / 7425 tests, typecheck, lint. Demo reloaded (the demo loader changed). Verify on perolehan_verify (demo reloaded first; a leftover row from the previous harness, InsuranceRequirement harness_req_car, removed; throwaway harness through the real acts, deleted; setup edits stated and restored: demo_rule60's approver, the S.O. rows' domain, demo_mx1's start and demo_rule60's clause each changed and put back; demo_con3's supervision wiped and the contract set STAMPED for D2; the verify demo reloaded afterwards): 13 of 13 as expected — S1 another office refused, nothing written; S2 no S.O. row refused; S3 no matrix on 29 Sep refused naming the day; S4 same office corrected, the assignment moved to 29 Sep with the re-resolved clause; S5 the move audited with resolvedAgainOn and the reason; D2a no S.O.: the preview's blocker and the act both say "No S.O. is resolved"; D2b Resolve the S.O.; D2c then the preview offers the drawer and the act records possession; C1 a WC figure with its reason; C2 cleared, the reason cleared (audited before/after); C3 a later figure with no reason refused naming the term; C4 with a new reason saved; C5 the clearing in the award's audited UPDATE. - 2026-10-03 · 6.13 · Code-review item 10 ruled: the self-help premium (premiumPaid) and the nominated party sum (partySum) are Internal, not SULIT. NFR-SEC-002's automatic SULIT classes are selected-tender information, the estimates, and evaluation and tender-certification information; a post-award premium charged back to the contractor and a nominated sub-contract sum are neither, and are figures of the same kind as the contract sum shown on /projects (unlike BD-612-24's arithmetic variance, which reveals the tender figure before correction). No SULIT chip, not added to the SULIT-field registry. Question to RSAJ: whether they classify post-award contract financials more widely. Spec: NFR-SEC-002, 6.12 Q2.
- 2026-10-03 · 6.13 · Code-review item 15 / BD-613-2 ruled: the departure is overruled. An award sum insured stated otherwise than the figure the frozen Appendix governs (works: the awarded value plus the Appendix's uplift; the Appendix's public liability and workmen's compensation minimums) is an Appendix difference under 64-C4: it needs an audited reason, is shown with both figures, and refuses issue until it has one, through the existing award-Appendix difference reason path. Where the Appendix states no figure there is nothing to differ from, so no reason is needed (said, not compared, as BD-64-40). The rest of BD-613-2 is approved. Fix in the current round: its own commit flagged 6.11, new tests only (below, above and equal to the minimum; Appendix silent), no existing test weakened. Spec: FR-CON-005, FR-PAY-014, 64-C4, ADR 0021 §2, AGENTS.md rule 2.

- 2026-10-03 · 6.13 · Part A run 1 (steps 0–2) built, 14d2bf6..57cf2c0, for the product owner's ruling after code review. BD-613 items, each the builder's call where the plan or the sources were silent:
  - BD-613-1 (step 0(a), interface re-check, divergences from plan §0.1/§0.2): `baselineFor(versions, day)` takes the version rows, not (contract, on); a Certificate carries templateId, pinnedAt, openedAt/openedOn, issuedAt and issueSnapshot beyond §0.2; `openCertificate(tx, l, …, now, today)` runs inside the raising act's own transaction; the sequence is taken at issue; DRAFT goes to SIGNING with no routing type; the S.O.'s blocks refused everyone until supervision (BD-614-12); 6.4's empowered officers and AppendixVariationLimit are read as assumed from the frozen Appendix; 6.5's HolidayCalendar is as built; 6.15's Supplier is reached through Award.vendorId; ContractType had no delegation variant (added, own migration); ACTIVE is reachable only from STAMPED (6.12's transitions), so possession needs a stamped contract; AuthorityMatrix.boundaryInclusive is non-null; the document store had no PIPE_LOG/AS_BUILT/PROGRAMME/DIARY_PHOTO purposes (each added in its own migration).
  - BD-613-2 (64-C4-b): three award columns (works, public liability, workmen's compensation); contractor's and erection all risks count as works; the works minimum is the awarded value plus the Appendix's uplift (none without one); a sum stated nowhere is said and not compared, and does not block; the best verified policy of each kind is judged; the award's own figure needs no reason.
  - BD-613-3 (612-C1): the correctable terms are LAD, DLP, retention and completion; the contract sum is excluded (its own act); a correction needs a baseline version (NO_BASELINE_VERSION); the award row is updated under an updatedAt guard and an AwardTermCorrection row is kept; the certificate page's "before execution" note reads the pinned Contract Date.
  - BD-613-4 (612-C2): History reads stored labels through the maps at read time (stored rows never rewritten), including 6.2's demo estimate events and the supplier's judged-under values; a fragment no map covers reads as the record type's name.
  - BD-613-5 (step 1): EDGE_UNDECIDED cannot arise from data while boundaryInclusive is non-null; the pure resolver reads null as ruled and the matrix screen states each percentage edge's side.
  - BD-613-6: existing callers of the engine do not pass contractTypeCode (a row confined to a type is skipped by them); 6.13's callers (the S.O.) do.
  - BD-613-7: the EOT and completion instrument twins wait for Part B's kinds; only sumAdjustmentTriggers is typed now.
  - BD-613-8 (614-C1): the money-bearing kinds are INTERIM_PAYMENT, ADVANCE_PAYMENT, VARIATION_APPLICATION, VARIATION and SUM_ADJUSTMENT; demo_hct4 was given the approved route demo_hri3 so it stays signable.
  - BD-613-9 (the signature hook, against accepted 6.12): the employer's signature resolves the S.O. on the issue in force, at the contract sum, the method it was let by and its type, and records it effective from the Contract Date; with no single S.O. the signature is refused (SO_UNRESOLVED), naming the matrix. A contract executed before 6.13 gets a one-off "Resolve the S.O." effective from the day it is recorded, never back-dated.
  - BD-613-10 (614-C4): the delegation letter opens no approval route (its block is the S.O.'s own), so 614-C4 moves to step 3 with the interim certificate, the first certificate that opens one.
  - BD-613-11 (who may be recorded): the S.O. is any holder of the office on the day or someone acting in it under a whole-office delegation (6.1); a representative is a delegate whose letter is issued on or before the day and not revoked by it (the revocation day is the first day it no longer holds) and whose scope lists the act; the special meeting's chair, early action, self-help and the S.O.'s own diary endorsement are the S.O.'s only; possession, programme approval and directions are the S.O.'s or the Principal Representative's; a superior officer's endorsement is recorded as named (no reporting lines yet).
  - BD-613-12: planned progress between two programme points is read on a straight line; In Delay compares exactly in integers, and exactly on the line is not below it (60.9% of 87% at 70% is not In Delay; 60.8% is). A lapsed date for completion with the work incomplete is In Delay whatever the percentage.
  - BD-613-13 (against accepted 6.14): the SO block is held by the S.O. office's holders on the day (and those acting in it); SO_REP by the Principal Representatives in force; RECOMMENDING_OFFICER resolves nobody until the interim certificate (step 3) says who recommends, and the block says so.
  - BD-613-14: possession freezes the start-of-work reading (BD-612-22) on the possession row and in the POSSESSION version's snapshot; recordPossession is the only ACTIVE writer and the lock-order scans name it.
  - BD-613-15: a delegation needs the S.O.'s recorded satisfaction with a note, a scope of at least one act, the contract type's letter variant and both forms set; one live delegation per person; never a representative delegating; it holds from the letter's certificate date; a void letter is not in force.
  - BD-613-16: one diary entry a day; an entry written after its day says why; missing days are shown, not blocking; a disagreement is stored and marked escalated to the S.O. (ASSUMED path), with no route.
  - BD-613-17: self-help records a PendingDeduction on the policy; deducting it is the interim certificate's (step 3).
  - BD-613-18: early action records its branches only, each saying where it opens (extensions of time and termination with Part B; liquidated damages with step 3's chain).
  - BD-613-19 (B1, OD-A8 A, ADR 0025): parties are totals only, recorded on an executed contract, with an optional sum, direct-payment answer and provenance; retired with a reason, never deleted.
  - BD-613-20: a handover pins the live versions of its set; PROGRAMME attachments open from execution, PIPE_LOG, AS_BUILT and DIARY_PHOTO once the contract is active.
  - BD-613-21 (OD-A7): the re-resolution switch is stored and shown; nothing writes RE_RESOLVED until an issued adjustment exists (step 4); unset warns on the Team tab and on every S.O. block.
  - BD-613-22: the demo's In Delay percentage is an invented 75 (never the customer's 70); the demo runs on demo_con3; the back-dated chain on a further contract waits for step 5.
  - BD-613-23: the supervision trail labels days in words and names its fields so the contract's History never reads them as codes (a party's type, a direction's text).
  - BD-613-24: possession judges the contract's state before the person recorded for it, so a contract not stamped says so first.

(1) R-8 69b7dd8 (stricter only): the re-review's harness evaded the write scan 14 ways of 16; now all 16 are self-tests and caught, with more of each kind: every Prisma 7 write op (updateManyAndReturn, createManyAndReturn) on a certificate-family model, optionally chained or on a destructured handle; any model handle not followed directly by `.op(` (alias, bracketed op, passed on, destructured; one exemption, a word map's `UNSET.certificate`, named); inside any Prisma write, every relation the schema types as a certificate-family model (read from schema.prisma: certificates, certificateSignatures, certificateOverrides, certificateDocuments, awardSheets, awardRoutingSheets, routingChecks, routingSheet, certificate, overrides; documents and signatures, which other models also use, under a family write) with a write key anywhere inside it or a value the scan cannot read; moveTo called directly by name only (never aliased, bound, called through .call or exported); raw SQL read whole with comments blanked (the shared lexer gains a comments-only mode, existing behaviour unchanged) so a multi-line, schema-qualified, MERGE, TRUNCATE, COPY, ALTER or DROP write is caught, Prisma.raw is flagged, and in any file naming a certificate table as an SQL identifier every raw call must be allow-listed by function and exact text (the certificate's lock in withLockedCertificate); src/components is scanned. Stated limit, not claimed away: another model's write whose data is a variable is not seen into. The previous entry's "raw SQL however built" is corrected in place. (2) BD-614-21: 30d3c5a (Templates, flagged) (a) a template another module uses (DocumentTemplate's relations to the letter of acceptance, appointment letters, tender packs, the notice, the invitation, the addendum, the evaluation report, the Kertas Cadangan, or documents already issued from it; a test holds the list to the schema) takes no block or recipient, refused naming its uses, and its version never moves without a text change; the form's page says so; (b) a provenance or a note alone no longer moves a form's version (blockPinChanged, rowPinChanged); the templates list says what the version counts ("version N (its text)" or "(its text, signature blocks and distribution list)"); 0d177a0 (c) recordSignature takes FOR SHARE on the template row (after the certificate's lock) before reading the form, and every edit locks it FOR UPDATE, so a pin never mixes versions. (3) Breadcrumbs 49f9f20 (shared top bar, flagged): crumbs.test.ts now walks every page.tsx (unnamed and named) and holds every crumb to an existing page and none to "Details"; fixed the dead /awards/[id]/print, /sourcing/[id]/evaluation, /label, /opening crumbs (each now the exercise's tab that holds it: Evaluation, Sales, Bid opening; a print crumb is the page itself), "Details" on /sourcing/[id] and its sub-pages, /sourcing/new, Configuration › Contract controls and Evaluation models (and a model), and the evaluation report and Kertas Cadangan naming the exercise at their own crumb instead of the exercise's. (4) 63d1df7 (shared audit labels, flagged): History and the export read the SULIT keys as "As computed", "Overridden to", "Supported up to" (EN and MS kept); ee477fe: a RECALLED route is its own state (ROUTE_RECALLED: "recalled before a decision; goes on only on a new route"), never "waiting for a decision", on the act, the page's blocker and the sheet; the award's sheet stays open for a new route; 7a40df5: the demo judges demo_aw5's received day before writing the sheet row, and takes every signed certificate's approved floor from its own frozen sheet; c241c62: ADR 0022 Amendment 1 (BD-614-20: the date corrected only while SIGNING). 960c58b: lint in two tests. Existing tests changed (stricter only): certificate-scan.test.ts's own self-tests from the fix round now expect the extra flags the stricter scan gives (a raw write also not allow-listed; the nested write's op name) and the lock example sits in its one allowed function, with the same lock elsewhere now asserted flagged; fix-routing.test.ts and sulit.test.ts gain tests. None weakened, skipped or deleted. Gates at 960c58b: npm test 423 files / 7,133 tests, typecheck, lint with check-ui clean. No migration. Demo wiped (--remove-dependents) and reloaded on perolehan. R-5 e7c5603: `receivedFaults` takes `routeBounds` and refuses a received day after the first route opened (AFTER_ROUTE_OPENED) or after any decision (AFTER_DECISION), the day itself allowed, on a certificate's sheet and the award's; the negative-elapsed "met" is pinned in a test (verify 16–17, 24–25). R-6 e7c5603 + b2fcbcf + c5c3664 (flagged 6.11): the award's sheet refuses a start, a check or a received-day edit once the latest appointment route is approved or refused (AWARD_ROUTE_DECIDED) or the award is cancelled; a resubmitted route reopens it; the tab shows it closed in the act's words (verify 22–23, 26). R-7 b13c7cc: the override's figures are audited as certificateComputedAmount / certificateOverrideAmount and a block's cap as signatureSupportedUpTo, registered in sulit-fields.ts; the contract's and the certificate's History chip them, the export withholds them (sulit.test.ts on the export's bytes; verify 21). R-8 af8cc75 (stricter only): `scanCertificateWrites` credits every certificate-row write to its true enclosing function against a per-function allow-list; data an inline literal with keys read quoted, shorthand or plain; each protected key (status, the date and its correction, number, sequence, issuedAt, issueSnapshot, routingSnapshot, the pinned form, the received day, the void) with named writers, so a direct status "ISSUED" is caught; moveTo's status a literal from its one listed function and its patch judged per status (routingSnapshot seen through moveTo, passed literally at its single call site); nested writes (first write key only), the model taken by name, raw SQL on one line (tagged, Prisma.sql, a string in a variable, an interpolated table) and any raw call with a non-template argument flagged [corrected 2026-10-03 by the residual round above: this scan was not airtight, the re-review evaded it 14 ways of 16; "raw SQL however built" overstated it]; the demo's writers pinned by function; lock order extended to awards/[id]/routing-actions.ts and award-sheet.ts; self-tests for each evasion. recordSignature's certificate write became one inline literal so the scan reads it. R-9 eec1398: the date is corrected only while SIGNING; ISSUED refuses (ISSUED_DATE_FINAL), VOID refuses, before the dating block NOT_DATED; the drawer is offered only while signing (verify 1–3, 10). R-10 eec1398 + c1ab7d6: an outsider's day, and the correction, are refused before the certificate was opened (BEFORE_OPENED) or before its route was approved (BEFORE_APPROVED) (verify 5, 7, 19–20). BD-614-9: going to signing needs DATE (1), the route APPROVED and every check of the current round; a certificate with no routing type goes from DRAFT straight to signing. BD-614-12: S.O., S.O. representative and recommending-officer blocks are resolved from the contract's supervision, which 6.13 (FR-SUP-001) records; until then such a block refuses, saying the appointment is not recorded, and no demo form uses one. BD-614-13: the date correction follows 6.12 OD-1 A (earlier only, a reason, never before the latest day a block printed before the dating block was signed); the issued text keeps the day it was issued with. BD-614-14: "distributed" means every mandatory copy has gone; acknowledgements outstanding are shown and do not undo it. - 2026-10-03 · 6.12 · Ruling Q1 (BD-612-23) · Keep: a baseline correction refuses a value the award, the Appendix or the bill states, or a recorded rule derives; only ENTERED values (and the Contract Date by its own act) are corrected. Not allowed: an officer's figure over a stated value "with both shown", because it would rank an entered figure above the Letter of Acceptance and leave the award and the baseline disagreeing. Fix now: STATED_NOT_CORRECTED's words (EN+MS) say a stated value recorded wrongly cannot yet be corrected and stop pointing to variations (a variation changes the terms, it does not fix a record; AGENTS.md rule 2); a pinned sentence that changes is faithful to this ruling; ADR 0021 Amendment 1 records the narrowing of §1's CORRECTION and the intended exit. Carry 612-C1: a reasoned, audited correction of the award's recorded terms after issue (6.11's updateAwardTerms refuses after issue), writing a CORRECTION version sourced AWARD and shown "corrected from …"; home ruled at 6.13 planning (6.11 follow-up or 6.13 Part A); it must land before any certificate reads a baseline in production. Spec: FR-CON-014, ADR 0021 §1, §2, §4, OD-5 A, AGENTS.md rule 2.

- 2026-10-03 · 6.12 · BD-612-23..29 (fix-round decisions, for ruling) · BD-612-23 (R-3, supersedes BD-612-9's "any value"): a baseline correction refuses a group the frozen inputs state (the Letter of Acceptance, the Appendix, the bill) or a recorded rule derives, naming it, rather than keeping the original source beside a corrected figure: ADR 0021 puts the award over the Appendix over the bill and keys only what nothing states, so an officer's correction over a stated value would rank an entered figure above the award; a change to the terms is 6.13's (variations and adjustments) and writes its own version. Groups entered at baseline are corrected as before, ENTERED with the provenance and reason. BD-612-24: an arithmetical correction's tendered and corrected figures are priced bid figures, SULIT as 6.8's correctedPrice is, registered as tenderedLineAmount / correctedLineAmount (specific keys, not "tendered"/"corrected"); the check's variance in the completion trail is not registered (it is the difference OD-3 A says is shown beside the sum on the Overview); for ruling whether it should be. BD-612-25 (S-1): numberedOn is the organisation's day as stored by the issue act, so the issue year is read from the stored day (dayOf on it would move a west-of-UTC day back); the year note and the December/January flag apply to SERIES numbers only, since an indent's own number states no issue year. BD-612-26 (R-6): a retired row is not re-dated by retiring it again (refused, naming the day); "retires from" shows a scheduled retirement. BD-612-27: the legal comments a return stands on are those attached from the review's sending to its decision (the window decideLegal accepts), by version, whether or not comments were also typed; attachNewVersion still moves the pin, as for every other reliance. BD-612-28 (M-1): a Contract Date correction on a contract executed before this round reads the signature days from the signature rows (single writer, never updated). BD-612-29 (S-2): the shared fix wraps every list badge wider than its column; /projects' three longer statuses now take two lines in its 200px column at 1280 and 1440 rather than any list's width changing. Spec: as above.

- 2026-10-03 · 6.12 · BD-612-1..22 (build decisions, for ruling) · BD-612-1 (step 0): §0.1 re-checked against the as-built code. 6.4's Appendix is read through frozenTenderOf and the approved pack's Appendix (first and subsequent interim minimums, days to honour, sections); 6.2's prime cost and provisional sums are the totals frozen when the bill the approved pack pinned was finalised; the first-interim floor and percentage are TenderDocumentPolicy.firstCertificateFloor/Pct; the LAD a day is 6.4's ladDaily (half up). Pre-6.4 exercises (all three demo contracts) have none of these and key them (OD-5). No divergence needing a re-ruling. BD-612-2: starting preparation refuses, naming each, where a setting formation cannot do without is unset (legal office, the agreement's signatory condition, the controlled-copy stamp, at least one copy recipient, the basis's "let on a bill" answer, its document parts); so no contract ever holds a frozen-unset rule it cannot leave. The front-loading ratio may be unset: the check is then the officer's judgement, said so. BD-612-3: which checks a basis takes is read from 6.2's ContractBasis.requiresBq (true: rate uniformity; false: the Schedule of Rates' adjustment; arithmetic and front loading always), frozen at the start; no new column. A services basis with no schedule records that check complete with a note. BD-612-4: front loading lists rates strictly above the frozen multiple; a rate exactly at it is listed apart "for judgement" (no inclusive switch is configured, so neither reading is chosen); a note is required whenever anything is listed or no ratio is set; read exactly in integers. BD-612-5: the contract goes to the legal office only with the assembly, reconciliation and checks complete; they are frozen while it is there and once endorsed, and open again when it is returned. BD-612-6: the contractor's signature needs what the RSAJ one needs before it (legally endorsed, the gate met, the resolution live): FR-CON-008 says the signature action. BD-612-7: confirming the baseline needs the readiness gate as 6.11 judges it (verified, stricter than FR-PAY-014's "lodged"). BD-612-8: "first ready to sign" for the Contract Date correction is the latest of the endorsement day, the instrument's receipt, each verification and the WC check day, and the resolution's attaching day (the organisation's day), frozen with the signature. BD-612-9: a baseline correction may correct any value (its source becomes ENTERED, with the officer's provenance and the reason); the Contract Date is corrected only by its own act. BD-612-10: a correction or rate line can be taken off, with a reason, only while its check is incomplete (the trail keeps it). BD-612-11: the estimate rate is SULIT (registered as estimateRate): shown with the chip, kept out of the trail. BD-612-12: the copy recipients and stamp words are frozen at preparation, not at issue; the consulting-engineer answer locks once a copy is issued. BD-612-13: the Configuration screen offers the signatory condition as the award_signatory rows that name no instrument, by their labels; no code is shown. BD-612-16: /projects derives "awaiting conditions" and "ready to sign" for at most 200 legally endorsed contracts. BD-612-17: the contract's History reads its own rows and every row written under "Contract <number> ·". BD-612-21: carry 64-C4-b (the award's own insurance terms) is not in the approved 6.12 plan and is not built; it is carried, for ruling where it belongs (a 6.11 follow-up or 6.13 Part A). BD-612-22: the type's startRequiresSecurities behaviour is not read at signing: the readiness gate is the award's own bond decision (bond required, or not with its reason) and the configured insurance requirements, which already bind every signature; a type that starts without securities is a 6.13 possession question. - 2026-10-02 · 6.15a · BD-615-2..17 (build decisions, for ruling) · BD-615-2: entity types and countries are the register's own list (SupplierListValue), not classification taxonomies (step 0: a supplier list would appear as an exercise classification). BD-615-3: a route names its supplier record from the route (ApprovalInstance.supplierApplicationId, standingRequestId, specialPermissionId; one CHECK says a SUPPLIER route names exactly one, any other none), not the plan's approvalInstanceId @unique on the record: a refused route leaves the matter open to be sent again, so a record may carry several routes; a blacklisting is asked for as a StandingRequest whose route must approve before the StandingEvent is recorded from it (the plan's event.approvalInstanceId could not exist before its route). BD-615-4: a supplier route's offices are held organisation-wide (supplierHolders); a delegation with a value ceiling is not counted (a matter with no value cannot be judged against a ceiling); a committee step on a supplier rule is refused (HAS_COMMITTEE_STEP) and the three domains are never tabled. BD-615-5: a document is verified only with its scan attached (SCAN_REQUIRED). BD-615-6: a contact's name, designation, email and telephone are registered SULIT fields (History chips them, the audit export withholds them) while the screens carry "not restricted until sign-in" (condition 2). BD-615-7: eligibility reads NOT_ASSESSED, never PASS, where 6.4 states a requirement's qualifiers in words only (no structured needs reach a requirement yet; the comparison by configured rank, any-of and all-of is built and tested in src/lib/suppliers/eligibility.ts, how structured needs reach a requirement is held for ruling with BD-615-1), where no document type records the body, where the exercise does not state whether it is restricted, and where the lapse rule is unset with a mandatory document expired. BD-615-8: the window check counts the verified chain together (a verified renewal starting the day after its predecessor ends carries the cover on); a renewal lodged and not verified makes a lapse inside the window NOT_ASSESSED, not FAIL. BD-615-9: StandingEvent.endsOn is the last blacklisted day (inclusive; free from the day after, BD-DS-10 arithmetic); a lift is its own request on supplier_blacklist (cond BLACKLIST_LIFT); REGISTRATION_CANCELLED is in the vocabulary and read by standing and the sale check, but no a2 act writes it (6.13 Part B or later). BD-615-10: the reinstatement checklist is one line per document the suspension was imposed for, each ticked as seen verified (no configured checklist; the rule is the documents). BD-615-11: at a sale, a linked party's registration and suspension checks are the register's (the officer's attestation is not used for it); a cancelled registration fails the registration check, a suspension (by hand or on lapse) or blacklisting the suspension check; the eligibility verdict is frozen on the sale and does not itself refuse it. BD-615-12: OD-6's consequence reuses ELIMINATE / REPORT; NOT_ASSESSED at an opening is shown, not a ground; the consequence is read from the register's policy until the opening is finalised and frozen then in the opening's policySnapshot (ineligible), audited. BD-615-13: a special permission needs the party linked (it is read by the supplier's eligibility); the notice's offer is a new window item (ExerciseWindow.specialPermissionOffered, null = not stated, which refuses); granted at once where only bodies outside the organisation grant it, after its route where the organisation's own approval is asked; it relaxes the grade or the field codes only. BD-615-14: the register's standing rules are their own act and audit label (Supplier register · standing rules), apart from the verification notice; reminder offsets come from the NotificationRule supplier.document.expiring, shown and never sent. BD-615-15: standing is never stored; the register list and the record's header show the derived standing (OD-7's exact wording where it applies). BD-615-16: demo ids beyond the plan's list, each grepped free first: demo_vnd7, demo_vapp7/7r, demo_vct7, demo_ai127/128 (vnd7's registration and pending renewal), demo_vscan*, demo_vdoc*, demo_n21 (series), demo_f6 (format), demo_nr7 (reminder rule), demo_rule120–122, demo_vsr1 (blacklisting request); 6.5's demo sx18 notice offers special permission (own flagged commit). BD-615-17: an award to a supplier who is suspended or blacklisted is not refused by 6.11 in a2 (deferred: the plan names the sale and the opening hooks only). Spec: FR-VND-001–004, 007–011, FR-ADV-010, 017, FR-BID-008 (paper), FR-TRM-017, FR-AWD-009, INT-03, OD-1..OD-10, conditions 1–6, AGENTS.md rules 1, 2 and 5.

- 2026-10-02 · 6.15a · BD-615-1 (step 0, for ruling) · The plan's interfaces re-checked against the as-built 6.4–6.8, 6.9 and 6.11 (plan §0.3). Names that moved are used as built (RegistrationBody labelEn/labelMs/qualifierLabelEn; the restriction is SourcingExercise.restrictedTender worded by TenderDocumentPolicy; recordSale under inWindow; OpenedBid's frozen findings). Also: entity type and country are not TaxonomyValue reuse (classification reads every taxonomy, the 64-C1 lesson) but a SupplierListValue table; FR-VND-001's "Bumiputera status" is the configured restriction-evidence document, not a column. Condition (5) checked: perolehan holds 17 parties and 7 awards, none with a vendorId. ADRs 0012–0018 claimed, 0019/0020 free and written (0020 Proposed, docs only). Spec: FR-VND-001, 003, 007, FR-DOC-005, OD-2, condition (1), (5).

- 2026-10-02 · Phase 2 close-out · 68-C5 (storedDay on instants), presented for review. The carry's six sites were examined first: ApprovalInstance.openedAt is not an instant. Every act that opens a route writes the organisation's day at midnight UTC (dayToDate(today); the intake writes the received trail's first decision day; openSuccessor is given dayToDate(today)), and all 45 rows in perolehan were at midnight. storedDay(inst.openedAt) in recordNegotiationDecision (meeting-actions.ts:249), decideAppointmentStep (awards/actions.ts:592), recordNameDecision (sourcing/actions.ts:628), recordDecision (requisitions/actions.ts:1248), recordWindowChangeDecision (window/change-actions.ts:331) and recordTenderingDecision (documents/actions.ts:757) is therefore correct in every zone; replacing it with dayIn(openedAt, zone), as the carry asked, would move the opened day back a day west of UTC (midnight UTC is 20:00 the evening before in New York). Not changed; proved instead by dd00fa3 (flagged against accepted 6.1, 6.3, 6.4, 6.5, 6.8, 6.11; source scan: every approvalInstance.create writes openedAt from the organisation's day, the list of writers is complete, successors open on dayToDate(today), the six acts and the Approval tab read it with storedDay; mutation-checked). (A first attempt that switched meeting-actions.ts to the zone's day was dropped before review, unpushed, for this reason.) The whole-src grep then found real instants read as UTC days, each fixed in its own flagged commit with new tests: 279b9b0 dayOf(instant, timeZone), the organisation's day with no UTC fallback (null with no zone); 4062931 (6.2) a bill version's finalisedAt and a panel verification's clearedAt (new Date()) shown with formatOrgDay; 84e04c9 (6.4) the assurance confirmation's pack-started day (TenderPack.createdAt) fell back to its UTC day with no zone, now dayOf and refused TIME_ZONE_NOT_SET; 1a47cca (6.8) the evaluation's startedAt, the recommendation's formedAt and the report's issuedAt read with dayIn (UTC fallback) now dayOf, and renderProposalPaper, which printed the report's issued day into the frozen Kertas Cadangan with no refusal when the zone had been cleared after the report issued, now refuses TIME_ZONE_NOT_SET; fd3da01 (6.10) Meeting.minutedAt and CommitteeDecision.withdrawnAt passed to the trail as Dates (renderValue writes their UTC day) now written with formatOrgDateTime. Existing test changed (flagged, stricter): evaluation/org-day.test.ts's R-4 source scan requires dayOf where it required dayIn, and that dayIn is not used on those instants; its behaviour cases unchanged. Listed, not changed: storedDay/ISO-slice of day columns (dates typed or written with dayToDate) everywhere else; Meeting.minutedAt read with formatOrgDateTime on the sitting page (correct, an instant); the window, opening, bids and estimate dayIn calls are all guarded on a set zone. Spec: NFR-AUD-005, ruling §9.5, BD-32, AGENTS.md rule 2.

- 2026-10-02 · Phase 2 close-out · 68-C3 (award gate and the quotation ceiling), presented for review. bb38d5e (flagged against accepted 6.11 and 6.8): loadEvaluationGate reads the exercise's frozen regime, boundaryValue and boundaryOperator (ceilingOf) onto the issued recommendation; evaluationFindings asks awardCeilingFault, the same ceilingExclusion rule 6.8 Q1 uses, whenever the awarded value differs from the recommendation: above the ceiling VALUE_ABOVE_CEILING names the boundary and which side an exact value falls (GT still a quotation, GTE a tender); a quotation whose boundary was never recorded blocks as BOUNDARY_NOT_RECORDED; a tender has no ceiling here; a value equal to the recommendation is not asked again (Q1 judged it when formed and at the report). Opening, editing, submission and issue all read it through evaluationFindings; the award form shows it under Awarded value. The ceiling is optional in the pure gate (absent: not asked, BD-68-6) and the one producer always states it (source scan). New tests only (ceiling-gate.test.ts, worked example): GT and GTE at the boundary and one sen above, tender never, boundary unset, equal value not asked, wording without codes. Spec: FR-EVL-016, FR-EVL-019, E-9, 6.8 Q1, AGENTS.md rules 1 and 2.

- 2026-10-02 · Phase 2 close-out · 67-C5 (pathTo truncation), presented for review. Not unreachable: saveOrgUnit checks a unit's parent only when the unit is created, so a parent may be retired afterwards with a child still in force (the shared fixture's dept_orphan under div_gone; buildStructure reports it detached). 81ca660 (flagged against accepted 6.7): new wholePathTo returns pathTo's path only when it reaches a unit with no parent, otherwise none (a loop included); the opening's unitPath reads it, so a member whose path is broken blocks as UNIT_NOT_RECORDED (not in the structure in force), the wording adding "or a unit above it is not". pathTo and its other callers (approval holders, endorsements, the evaluation team's implementing-unit check, which a cut-short path can only ever block) are unchanged. New tests only (whole-path.test.ts); panel-unit.test.ts's prefix match unchanged. Spec: FR-CTE-011, C-6, R-6.

- 2026-10-02 · 6.8 · Build decisions (BD-68-1..17), for ruling. 1 Step 0 (plan §0.1): names used as built — evaluationSet, the frozen schedule's benchmark as the only estimate read, consentPartyId, publishFaults' one more input, the negotiation domain in the one route splitter; no rule changed. 2 EvaluationState is OPEN, ISSUED, SUPERSEDED, CANCELLED (no SUBMITTED: a stage's submission is its own row); Evaluation.policySnapshot freezes the score's places and their provenance at the start (E-5), the 6.6/6.7 freeze pattern. 3 Lock order extended at its end (requisition → exercise → window → openings → envelopes/bids → evaluations); the two acts that come from a sitting lock sitting → evaluations, never the exercise, so no two acts take the same locks in opposite orders (lock.ts). 4 The pin is stamped with the publication's own clock read and guarded on evaluationModelPinnedAt null (a second pin refuses CHANGED); a published or cancelled exercise takes no model choice. 5 The E-9 gate sits in evaluationFindings, and exerciseOpenForAward accepts every status past the opening, so an exercise published before models existed reads NEVER_EVALUATED (naming cancel and replace) rather than a status refusal; no C-8 test was rewritten. 6 The evaluation input is optional in the pure award gates (old fixtures unchanged); the one producer, awardFacts, always states it, held by a source scan (hooks.test.ts). 7 A draft award's edit asks only the faults a field can fix (fieldsOnly); the rest are judged at submission. 8 Configuration: children are audited against their model's id; one draft per model at a time; retiring keeps every pinned snapshot; a draft never issued may be discarded with a reason. 9 Which side of a pass mark or a band edge an exact value falls on is stated for each one; unset blocks the save or the issue, never defaults (AGENTS.md boundaryInclusive pattern). 10 The loop-back never refuses the committee's outcome: an unknown or legacy key, a TERMINAL outcome or an exercise not published through the window records the outcome and says why no version opens. 11 No inputs for a bid excluded or out of the running at the stage reading them (runningFaults, shared with assessAspect); found by the verify run. 12 The recommendation is refused while a negotiation is still with approval (NEGOTIATION_PENDING, the report's code): formed first, an approved negotiation could never be held; found by the verify run. 13 Demo ids: attendance demo_at64–67 (E-12 named at60–63, already 6.6's); document counter from 110; sx16's evaluation stamped 145 to 100 minutes before now, before demo_aw7 (90); demo_sx21 and demo_c7 per 67-C3. 14 The demo's "Return for re-clarification" (demo_nao1, 6.10's) keeps its legacy free-text stage "evaluation", so Configuration shows the legacy warning and demo_d43 stays PENDING (plan §6); the verify run re-keys it through Configuration and shows the loop. 15 The record's "Evaluation runs outside the portal" banner removed and the new exercise's "Sourcing runs outside the portal for now" subtitle reworded, both false once built (flagged 6.7, 6.3). 16 The demo reads an exercise's closing through loadExercise, not its own select, so the X-2 scan (closing-writers.test.ts) stays as accepted. 17 6.5's publications table scrolls inside its own frame at 1280 (as on demo_sx18 before 6.8); not changed. Questions for RSAJ: D1 (a model per method in use), OD-5, OD-10. Spec: FR-EVL-001–020, FR-CTE-007, 009, 013, E-1..E-13, OD-1..OD-10.

the two are the same instant for a LOCKED event). 3 An undecided late envelope does not block finalising the main opening (it is never openable there); the award gate holds it (6.6 LATE_UNDECIDED, then ADMITTED_UNOPENED once carried). 4 A panel is drawn from every seat in force (voting, alternates, the secretary), each resolved to its one holder; a vacant or contested seat holds nobody; the seat is snapshotted as its office, its label or its position. 5 Presence is fixed when the opening starts; an absent member neither authorises, initials nor signs; at least two present. 6 The independence rule excludes the named unit and every unit under it (path through the structure), beyond C-6's "the unit". 7 An invited exercise's bidder is checked against its letters of invitation (NOT_ON_INVITEE_LIST without one); the INVITEES reading counts letters, PURCHASERS distinct parties holding a live sale. 8 The opening committee, the unpurchased-bid consequence and the estimate's requirement are read live when the opening starts and frozen on it (not at publication as 6.6 CR-2); an unpurchased consequence unset at start but set since is recorded on the opening, audited, the first time a verdict needs it. 9 A verdict is only on eliminatory grounds; there is no free "other" rejection (a concern goes in the minutes); a changed checklist answer clears the verdict. 10 A correction asks every present member's initial again and has the price entered afresh (the drawer never shows it back). 11 A supplementary opening uses the main opening's checklist and rules; its panel rules are read when it starts. 12 One copy explanation per opening. 13 returnedByBidder unset on an approved pack has no exit (CR-2 precedent): finalising names Configuration › Tender documents; drafts refresh; the declaration is returned by definition. 14 A voluntary estimate's paper copy may be left unanswered (no comparison then); a required one must be answered. 15 An opening with no offer finalises (an empty box); the exit from a failed tender is cancellation (C-9). 16 The opening's settings sit on Configuration › Invitation (an Opening card with its own count, the window badge unchanged), the panel on each committee in its own drawer with a reason, the unit on the person (optional on the Person type so no fixture changes; the drawer comparator records the new field as decided). 17 The tab is "Bids and opening", but two 6.6 texts locked by existing tests still say "the Bids received tab" (committee/labels.ts LATE_TABLED_TEXT, the award's late-envelope fault); left unchanged, rewording to "the Received section of Bids and opening" would change committee/labels.test.ts and award/bids-gate.test.ts. 18 The print before finalising shows the offers read so far with the estimate sealed, watermarked "Not finalised" (the panel signs that paper; plan §5 said "frozen rows only"). 19 The paper copy's figure is audited under a new registered SULIT key, estimatePaperCopy. 20 Demo per OD-4 A: REPORT, so no demo offer is rejected (REJECT covered by tests and the verify run); demo_aw7 now names Contoh Bina (demo_pty2), carried, at its price as read; "Demo Network Builders" is a bidder named at opening. 21 History still names 6.6's envelope rows "Record" (a 6.6 gap, not changed); the opening's rows have their names. 22 The Grand Summary shows the English words on screen; the Malay ones are kept for a bilingual print. 23 The panel and who is present are two multi-selects in the start drawer (the shared multi-select posts newline-joined; the action splits it). 24 The demo wipe removes a box envelope with its opening (the CHECK) and clears an award's awardee link before the parties go.
